### [1] [OSIRIS-REx Lessons Learned Report: Insights from the Journey to Bennu and Back](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33214)
*Dante S. Lauretta,Heather L. Enos,C. W. V. Wolner*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: OSIRIS-REx成功从贝努小行星采集并返回超量原始样本，尽管面临诸多挑战；本报告总结了其在领导治理、成本进度风险管控、系统工程、任务运行、样品返回与分析、数据归档六方面的经验教训。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 总结OSIRIS-REx任务在复杂约束与意外挑战下仍超额完成目标的经验，为未来小天体采样返回任务提供可复用的管理与技术启示。

Method: 基于任务全过程实践，由项目领导层系统梳理六大关键领域（领导与治理、成本/进度/风险管理、系统工程、任务运行、样品返回/处理/分析、数据归档），结合具体问题与应对措施提炼共性规律和针对性建议。

Result: 识别出四项核心经验：PI主导的融合型组织结构提升决策效率；前期投入储备与进度余量以主动控险（如资助NFT备份导航）；继承硬件需同步传承验证数据与工程知识；污染防控、样品科学与数据管理须贯穿全周期。

Conclusion: OSIRIS-REx不仅是一项成功的科学采样任务，更是一次高韧性项目管理的范例；其经验强调‘人’（领导力与协同）、‘钱与时间’（弹性资源使用）、‘技术根基’（硬件与知识双继承）及‘责任延伸’（全周期科学与数据 stewardship）四大支柱对复杂深空任务的关键作用。

Abstract: OSIRIS-REx, NASA's third New Frontiers mission, was the first U.S. mission to return a sample from an asteroid. Proposed in 2010 for an \$811 million lifecycle cost, the mission was completed for \$828 million, returned more than \$52 million of Headquarters reserves unused, and delivered at least 121.6 g of pristine material from near-Earth asteroid (101955) Bennu in September 2023, more than twice the Level-1 requirement. It did so despite a surface far more rugged than predicted, the discovery of particle ejection, sampling in a regolith much weaker than expected, operations during the COVID-19 pandemic, and an off-nominal parachute sequence on Earth return. This report sets out the lessons learned by the mission's leadership across six areas: leadership and governance; cost, schedule and risk management; systems engineering; mission operations; sample return, curation and analysis; and data archiving. Recurring themes are that a PI-led structure integrating science, engineering and project management gives the discipline to make fast, informed decisions; that reserves and schedule margin spent early to retire risk, such as funding Natural Feature Tracking as a backup navigation system, buy the flexibility to absorb discovery; that heritage hardware reduces risk only when its verification and engineering knowledge are preserved; and that contamination knowledge, sample science and data stewardship are mission-long responsibilities, not post-flight tasks. Each section pairs background with specific recommendations for future sample-return and small-body missions.

</details>


### [2] [Modeling the LISA response in the time-frequency domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33215)
*Tomasz Kinowski,Christopher J. Moore*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种基于Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer（WDM）变换的LISA响应时间-频率域建模方法，使用JAX实现向量化计算，高效处理信号批次，白噪声失配达~10^{-9}，验证了该方法在精度与效率上的可行性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 为构建端到端的时间-频率域LISA数据分析框架，需一种高效、精确的LISA响应建模方法。

Method: 采用Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer（WDM）变换直接在时间-频率域建立LISA响应模型，并利用JAX进行向量化实现，支持批量信号快速评估。

Result: 所提模型实现了约10^{-9}量级的白噪声失配，精度媲美现有单臂响应实现，且具备高效批量计算能力。

Conclusion: LISA响应可在时间-频率域中高精度、高效率地建模，为时间-频率域LISA数据分析提供了关键支撑。

Abstract: Time-frequency methods are a promising approach to LISA data analysis. This paper presents a model for the LISA response directly in the time-frequency domain using the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) transformation. The implementation is vectorized using JAX and can efficiently evaluate the response for batches of signals, achieving white-noise mismatches of $\sim 10^{-9}$ compared to existing implementations of the LISA one-arm response. Our results demonstrate that the LISA response can be modeled accurately and efficiently in the time-frequency domain, providing an important component of an end-to-end time-frequency LISA analysis framework.

</details>


### [3] [RIFT for the O4 era: operating points, calibration marginalization, and cross-code validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33239)
*R. O'Shaughnessy,A. Jan,J. Lange,R. Mechum,N. Malagon*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文介绍了RIFT参数推断算法在O4观测期间的多项改进，提升了其单事件推断能力、与其他代码的兼容性及鲁棒性，并解决了与其他主流推断代码之间的似然差异问题。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 提升RIFT算法在引力波单事件参数推断中的效率、鲁棒性与跨代码可比性，适配O4阶段更灵敏的探测器数据。

Method: 对RIFT算法进行多方面升级：(a)增强校准边缘化能力；(b)优化似然函数的内部、输出及时间插值表示；(c)引入新坐标系描述横向自旋和偏心平均近点角；(d)调整运行点以适配最强信噪比信号；并加入自动化波形对称性检查以识别上游模型退化。

Result: 显著缩小了RIFT与其他主流推断代码间的似然差异；实现了与asimov接口的端到端可复现验证；提升了对复杂参数相关性的稳健推断能力。

Conclusion: 这些改进使RIFT在O4阶段更可靠、兼容且高效，为当前及未来引力波数据分析提供了坚实工具支持。

Abstract: The Rapid Iterative FiTting (RIFT) parameter inference algorithm provides a simulation-based-inference approach to efficient, highly-parallelized parameter inference for GW sources. In this paper, we describe improvements deployed during preparations for and analysis of O4 observations (versions 0.0.17.3--0.0.18.0) that broaden RIFT's reach for single-event inference with contemporary instruments. We provide additional options to increase compatibility and comparability with other codes, notably including (a) enhancing RIFT's capabilities for calibration marginalization; (b) managing our likelihood's internal, output, and time-interpolated representation; (c) introducing new coordinates for (transverse) spin and eccentric mean anomaly, enabling more robust inference of parameters with subtle and complex correlations to others, and (d) revised operating point choices better suited to the loudest, clearest signals. We identify and reconcile the most substantial sources of head-to-head likelihood differences between RIFT and other production inference codes, and describe automated waveform symmetry checks that detect upstream model regressions before they bias inference. Using the previously-reported RIFT/asimov interface for reproducible settings consistent with past work, we demonstrate these capabilities end to end.

</details>


### [4] [When precision exceeds fidelity: systematic error quantification for inference with misspecified simulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33282)
*M. J. Yantovski-Barth,Hengyue Zhang,Nolan Smyth,Aristeidis Amvrosiadis,Martin Bureau,Yashar Hezaveh,Laurence Perreault-Levasseur*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种基于模型影响图（influence map）的新方法，用于识别和校正模拟器系统性偏差对参数推断的影响，特别适用于贝叶斯推断中模型误设导致的系统误差主导情形。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 随着科学数据精度提升，参数推断的主要不确定性来源从测量噪声转向模拟器本身的系统性误差；传统贝叶斯证据加权平均会低估此类系统误差，尤其当模型不同部分相互补偿误设时。

Method: 定义并高效估计参数对数据扰动的局部响应——即‘影响图’（通过已有后验样本上的前向模拟计算）；将数据与模拟之间的残差沿各模型的影响图传播，从而分离并定位各类系统性偏差源；最后实施一阶偏差校正。

Result: 在超大质量黑洞质量推断任务中，发现误差主要由模型系统性偏差主导；识别出所有模型共有的简化假设，利用影响图传播相应残差并校正后，显著提升了各模型内不同观测间质量推断结果的一致性，同时保留了模型间的差异以表征剩余系统性不确定性。

Conclusion: 影响图提供了一种可计算、可解释的工具，使系统性偏差诊断与校正成为可能，从而提升高保真科学建模中参数推断的可靠性与可重复性。

Abstract: As scientific datasets grow in complexity and precision, the primary source of uncertainty in parameter inference shifts from measurement noise to systematic errors within the simulators themselves. In this setting, Bayesian evidence concentrates on whichever misspecified model best approximates the data. Evidence-weighted model averaging can then underestimate systematic uncertainty when one model component compensates for the misspecification of another component. We posit that in this case, the key question is how each model responds to discrepancies between simulator and data. We show that the local response of an inferred parameter to perturbations in the data --- the model's influence map --- can be estimated efficiently from forward evaluations of the simulator at existing posterior samples. Propagating discrepancy residuals through these influence maps enables individual sources of systematic bias to be isolated. Applied to independent observations used to infer the mass of a supermassive black hole, we find that the error is dominated by model systematics. We identify a simplification shared by all models, propagate the discrepancies through each model's influence map, and apply a first-order correction of the resulting biases. This substantially improves the cross-observation consistency of the inferred black-hole mass within each model while preserving the between-model spread that represents remaining systematics.

</details>


### [5] [line racer: Rapid Calculation of Exoplanetary Radiative Opacities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33290)
*David B. Hägele,Paul Mollière,Michiel Min*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: line racer 是一个用于高效计算高分辨率分子和原子光谱不透明度的Python软件包，支持大规模并行计算，并兼容主流大气建模与反演工具。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 精确掌握系外行星和褐矮星大气中各种分子和原子的光谱特征，是开展详细大气研究的前提；而这些特征依赖于随温度、压力变化的不透明度计算，需在宽泛条件下高效完成。

Method: 开发了名为 line racer 的 Python 软件包，基于大型分子与原子谱线列表，支持高度可定制、硬件适配（多核/多节点）的高分辨率不透明度计算。

Result: 实现了高效并行化的不透明度计算能力，输出格式兼容主流大气建模与参数反演工具（如 petitRADTRANS、ATMO 等）。

Conclusion: line racer 为系外行星与褐矮星大气研究提供了灵活、高效、可扩展的光谱不透明度计算新工具，显著提升了大气模型构建与观测解释的精度与效率。

Abstract: Detailed studies of exoplanet and brown dwarf atmospheres rely on precise knowledge of the spectral features of possible atmospheric species. These features are a result of the interaction of different molecules and atoms with the radiation of the host star or the intrinsic thermal radiation of the object. The interaction is described by the opacity of a species, which determines how much light is absorbed at a given wavelength. The strength and shape of these opacities are very dependent on the temperature and pressure of the atmosphere, which is why they have to be calculated for a wide range of conditions. line racer is a Python package that enables computing high-resolution opacities from large molecular and atomic line lists in an effective manner. It offers users a wide range of options to customize opacity calculations to their needs and available hardware. The code is designed for efficient parallelization on multi-core and multi-node systems and can produce outputs compatible with popular atmospheric modeling and retrieval tools.

</details>


### [6] [Toward Automated Strong-Lens Modeling with Agentic Workflows: An Application to Euclid Q1 Lenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33363)
*Xiaoyue Cao,Ran Li,Yunlong Yin,Rui Li,Liang Gao*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文提出Lens-Orchestra，一个结合多模态大语言模型代理与物理建模的自动化强引力透镜分析流程，在Euclid Q1数据上实现无需人工干预的高质量建模，验证了其在大规模巡天中从宏观质量分布到小尺度暗物质结构研究的可行性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 宽视场巡天将发现大量强引力透镜，但传统建模依赖人工干预，效率低且难以扩展，亟需自动化、可靠、端到端的建模流程。

Method: 构建Lens-Orchestra自动化工作流，集成多模态大语言模型代理与多阶段物理建模（椭圆幂律质量分布、外部剪切、像素化源重建），支持自然语言指令启动，并包含残差筛查代理以识别亚结构候选体。

Result: 在185个Euclid Q1强透镜候选体上完成149次拟合，其中140个模型经人工评估可用（126个为高质grade-I拟合）；Einstein半径与已有模型高度一致（中位偏移+0.12%，散射2.73%）；层级推断得平均质量密度斜率2.002±0.009；残差代理成功引导两个亚结构候选体的建模分析。

Conclusion: 多模态智能代理与显式正向建模的耦合，可实现高精度、可扩展、端到端的强透镜物理分析，为未来大规模巡天提供可靠技术路径。

Abstract: Ongoing and forthcoming wide-field surveys will discover $O(10^5)$ galaxy-galaxy strong lenses, shifting the main bottleneck from detection to reliable physical modeling. Conventional forward-modeling pipelines still rely on case-by-case expert judgment and manual tuning to mask contaminants, initialize priors, diagnose unphysical fits, and reconfigure failed runs. We present Lens-Orchestra, an automated workflow coupling a team of multimodal large language model agents to multi-stage physical modeling with elliptical power-law mass profiles, external shear, and pixelized source reconstructions. Launched via natural-language instructions, it modeled 185 Euclid Quick Data Release (Q1) grade-A lens candidates with published mass models without human intervention, completing 149 fits. Independent human evaluation confirmed 140 usable models, including 126 grade-I fits with noise-level residuals (with only 6.0% of completed fits unacceptable), and endorsed all 36 halted runs. Across the 140 usable systems, effective Einstein radii match released Q1 models (+0.12% median offset; 2.73% typical scatter), while hierarchical inference on the 126 grade-I imaging-only fits yields a mean mass-density slope of $2.002 \pm 0.009$ (intrinsic scatter $0.074^{+0.007}_{-0.006}$). A dedicated residual-screening agent also guided the selection of two candidate low-mass perturbers for parametric and free-form substructure modeling; although neither was confirmed, this end-to-end search illustrates how automated screening focuses computationally intensive substructure analyses on a few promising systems. Coupling multimodal agents to explicit forward modeling thus enables accurate, survey-scale lens analysis from macro mass profiles to small-scale dark matter structure.

</details>


### [7] [Photometric Uncertainty Correction for Time-Domain Surveys: Vera C. Rubin Data Preview 2 Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33419)
*Konstantin Malanchev,Doug Branton,Sandro Campos,Neven Caplar,Mi Dai,Melissa DeLucchi,Olivia Lynn,Rachel Mandelbaum,Sean McGuire,Inar Timiryasov*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种基于非变源光变曲线的数据驱动方法，用于校准时域巡天（如Rubin观测站DP2）的测光误差，发现亮源误差被低估达5倍，并给出了mmag量级的误差下限修正。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 时域科学（如变源探测、超新星宇宙学）高度依赖准确的测光误差估计，但现有巡天报告的误差常存在系统性偏差，亟需数据驱动的校准方法。

Method: 利用非变源光变曲线构造均值-方差白化信号，通过将其分布与标准正态分布匹配来估计误差修正因子；该因子建模为流量、报告误差及观测元数据的函数。

Result: 在Rubin DP2数据上验证，亮源测光误差被低估最高达5倍；推导出直接成像和差分成像的测光误差下限约为10 mmag（深场降至6 mmag）；已将修正后的误差集成至DP2 HATS星表。

Conclusion: 所提方法可有效诊断并校准巡天测光误差，显著提升时域数据可靠性，适用于变源与非变源，并已实际应用于Rubin早期数据发布。

Abstract: Time-domain science, from variability detection to supernova cosmology, relies on accurate photometric uncertainties. We present a data-driven, catalog-level method to validate and improve the photometric uncertainties reported by time-domain surveys. The analysis is based on transforming light curves of non-variable objects into mean-variance whitened signals. We then estimate a correction to the uncertainties by matching the distribution of the whitened signals of all objects to the standard normal distribution, which they ought to follow when the uncertainties are correct. The correction can be modeled as a function of each measurement's flux, reported uncertainty, and available observational metadata, and can be applied to measurements of both non-variable and variable objects. Applied to the Rubin Observatory Data Preview 2 (DP2), the method shows that the uncertainties of bright sources are underestimated by up to a factor of five. The inferred corrections correspond to a magnitude error floor of about 10 mmag for both direct-image and difference-image photometry, decreasing to about 6 mmag in deeper fields. We provide the corrected uncertainties as part of the Rubin DP2 HATS catalogs.

</details>


### [8] [3D Shape Recovery of Human-Made Space Objects from Light Curves with Image Denoisers as Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33520)
*Daigo Kobayashi,Carolin Frueh*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种基于去噪器正则化的框架，用于从未解析光变曲线中恢复人造空间物体的三维凸形，显著提升了形状重建精度和鲁棒性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统方法在利用非负最小二乘和方向优化恢复扩展高斯图像（EGI）时会丢失曲面法向信息，难以准确表征弯曲表面，影响空间目标表征与态势感知。

Method: 将EGI表示为等距矩形网格上的面积图像，在随机旋转坐标系中使用带域适配DRUNet去噪器的即插即用ADMM算法进行恢复；再通过具有解析梯度的尺度不变支持优化与体积归一化，重构凸多面体。

Result: 在7种形状（含曲面与多面体）上，信噪比30 dB时，平均凸包交并比（IoU）从基线0.615提升至0.872，Hausdorff距离与Chamfer距离也大幅下降；噪声下（20 dB）IoU仅轻微下降至0.854，显示强鲁棒性。

Conclusion: 该去噪器正则化框架有效保留曲面几何信息，显著提升三维凸形重建精度与抗噪能力，适用于空间目标识别与任务支持。

Abstract: This study presents a denoiser-regularized framework for recovering the three-dimensional convex shapes of human-made space objects from unresolved light curves. These shape estimates support object characterization for space situational awareness and mission support. Conventional methods for recovering the extended Gaussian image (EGI) using nonnegative least squares and directional refinement can discard surface-normal information needed to represent curved surfaces. The proposed framework represents the EGI as an area image on an equirectangular grid and recovers it in a randomly rotated frame using the plug-and-play alternating direction method of multipliers with a domain-adapted DRUNet denoiser. It then reconstructs a convex polytope from the recovered area image using scale-invariant support optimization with analytical gradients and volume normalization. Across seven faceted and curved shapes, with 10 runs per condition, the proposed method increases mean convex-hull intersection over union (IoU) from 0.615 for the baseline to 0.872 at a signal-to-noise ratio of 30 dB. Mean Hausdorff distance decreases from 0.198 to 0.050, and mean Chamfer distance decreases from 0.167 to 0.041. All three metrics improve for six of the seven shapes. The proposed method also retains much of its accuracy under noise, with mean IoU decreasing from 0.881 for noiseless measurements to 0.854 at 20 dB.

</details>


### [9] [Superfit: A Supernova Spectral Classification Tool with Host-Galaxy Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33528)
*D. Andrew Howell,Alyona Kosobokova,Samantha Goldwasser,Ido Irani,Saanvi Tiwary,Ofer Yaron,Avishay Gal-Yam*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: Superfit 是一个开源的超新星光谱自动分类工具，最新版用 Python 重写并提供基于浏览器的 Web 界面，通过最小二乘拟合同时匹配暂现源与宿主星系光谱，支持红移、消光和宿主污染校正。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解决超新星光谱分类中需同时处理红移、星际/星系际消光及宿主星系污染的问题，并提升方法的可复现性、可验证性与用户可解释性，替代黑箱式机器学习方法。

Method: 采用最小二乘拟合方法，对用户上传的观测光谱，同步拟合暂现源（超新星）与宿主星系模板光谱；支持自定义光谱重采样、红移与消光范围、相位信息及模板子集限制；使用扩展的、含元数据的现代暂现源与星系模板库。

Result: 实现了功能完备的 Web 应用：支持参数配置、实时光谱可视化、拟合过程监控、交互式候选体排序与成分分解查看；新版显著提升了易用性、灵活性与可解释性。

Conclusion: Superfit 提供了一种透明、可控、物理直观的超新星光谱分类方案，适用于专业天文学家及教育场景，在自动化与可理解性之间取得良好平衡。

Abstract: Superfit is a an open-source tool for automated spectroscopic classification of supernovae that accounts for redshift, reddening, and host-galaxy contamination. Here we present the latest version, which has been rewritten in Python and is now available as a web-based application. A user uploads an observed spectrum, and Superfit uses a curated library of transient and galaxy spectra to perform a simultaneous fit of transient and host galaxy spectra, while adjusting for color and redshift. In contrast to machine-learning algorithms, Superfit uses a least squares fitting approach, which is reproducible, verifiable, and understandable to users. The user may specify the binning of the spectra, set redshift and reddening limits, phase information, and restrict host galaxy and supernova templates for fitting. A new, expanded template library includes modern transient subclasses and associated observational metadata. A modern browser-based interface allows users to configure fitting parameters, visualize spectra, monitor fitting progress, and interactively inspect and compare ranked candidate matches and their individual components. Abstract: The universe below 20 MHz remains one of space science's last unexplored frontiers, inaccessible from Earth due to its ionosphere and terrestrial radio frequency interference (RFI). We present the mission concept for the Moon Radio Interferometer for Science and Exploration (MoonRISE), an all-sky imaging, low-frequency radio telescope operating from 100 kHz-30 MHz from the lunar farside, the quietest radio environment in the inner Solar System. If realized, MoonRISE would be the first radio interferometer on the Moon and the first connected-element radio interferometer in space. Its science objectives are to (1) determine how solar wind turbulence drives plasma heating, acceleration, and space weather evolution in the inner heliosphere, by measuring the scattering of type III solar radio bursts between 2-20 solar radii; (2) constrain the density, structure, and variability of the lunar ionosphere and its effects on surface communication; and (3) resolve the plasma sources and magnetospheric dynamics that power Uranian Kilometric Radiation, observable for the first time since Voyager 2 in 1986. The same observations would yield all-sky maps of the low-frequency sky, subsurface structure at the landing site, and a characterization of the farside RF environment. The Radio Interferometer concept consists of nine 3.5 m dipoles embedded in a 1.5 km optical-fiber tether deployed by a rover, with power- and RF-over-fiber enabling RFI-free mapping. A rover-mounted Ground Penetrating Radar (300-2000 MHz) would measure regolith dielectric properties and bedrock depth to characterize the dipoles' RF performance. We describe the science objectives, a reference landing site in Lacus Luxuriae, a notional concept of operations, and the payload design and technology maturity. The concept builds on SunRISE and OVRO-LWA heritage and could serve as a basis for a future selected mission.

Abstract: We report a recurring spectral artifact identified in observations obtained with the integral field unit (IFU) of the Gemini Multi-Object Spectrograph on the Gemini North telescope (GMOS-N). Similar features were repeatedly identified in observations of different astronomical targets obtained between 2020 and 2022 using the R150 grating in the two-slit IFU mode. Taking advantage of the reproducibility of the artifact in the fiber and wavelength domains, we develop an empirical correction method in which the artifact is modeled as the product of a common spectral profile and a fiber-dependent amplitude distribution, with an overall normalization determined independently for each exposure. The artifact template is constructed from observations in which the affected fibers are largely separated from the astronomical source and subsequently applied to independent observations in which the artifact overlaps the source. The correction substantially suppresses the artificial spatial structure in the reconstructed images. Although the physical origin of the artifact remains unidentified, we present its observational characteristics and correction procedure to facilitate the identification and treatment of similar features by other GMOS-IFU users.

Key metrics include read noise, dark current, photo and dark signal non-uniformity, quantum efficiency, and window transmittance. The median read noise is 1.07\,e$^-$ (LN) and 2.79\,e$^-$ (HWC). Bias shows minor dark signal non-uniformity: 0.076\,e$^-$ (LN), 0.173\,e$^-$ (HWC). Pixel well depth reaches 15700\,e$^-$ (LN) and 50000\,e$^-$ (HWC), with dynamic ranges of 83.39\,dB and 85.05\,dB, respectively. Linearities are 99.93\,\% (LN) and 99.82\,\% (HWC) within the 5-95\,\% range of the saturation signal. Photo response non-uniformity is 0.076\,\% (LN) and 0.05\,\% (HWC). Dark current at 0\,$^\circ$C is 0.001\,\eps, (LN and HWC). On-sky tests show that, for our test telescope configuration, the HWC mode can be camera noise limited for specific magnitude ranges, while the LN mode remains free of such limitations, making it the preferred choice.

Abstract: We aim to carry out fast, spatio-temporally regularized inversions of full fields of view, optionally including the PSF of the telescope, for both photospheric and chromospheric spectral lines. We replace the forward model by fast, precise, and differentiable neural emulators trained on synthetic Stokes profiles. Neural surrogate models are used to emulate the Stokes profiles of the Fe I doublet at 6302 A, the Ca II line at 8542 A as well as the Si I line and He I multiplet at 10830 A. These emulators are then used in a fast inversion code that includes spatial regularization terms and can compensate for the aberrations of the telescope. The emulators are unbiased, with errors that are, in most cases, comparable to or below the noise of current observations in the polarization profiles. Inversions of large fields-of-view can be carried out in minutes in a off-the-shelf GPU, opening up the possibility of real-time analysis of large-scale solar observations. We provide a few illustrative examples. Emulators open up the option of inverting lines with very complex and time consuming forward models.

Abstract: Amorphous solid water (ASW) dominates interstellar ice mantles, but its porosity, density, optical response, and mechanical stability depend sensitively on how the ice is grown. Here, we present the characterization capabilities of COSPINU2, a cryogenic ultra-high-vacuum set-up designed to prepare and diagnose water ice films under controlled background and doser-assisted deposition conditions. The instrument combines a calibrated gas-handling line, near- and mid-infrared Fourier-transform infrared spectroscopy, HeNe interferometry, broad-beam optical imaging, and quadrupole mass spectrometry. We demonstrate how these complementary diagnostics provide a coherent description of ASW films grown between 10K and 150K. For background-grown ices, a dual-angle interferometric method is used to retrieve refractive indices near 632 nm, while near-infrared interference fringes provide thickness calibration. The results show that lower deposition temperatures and higher growth rates produce films with lower refractive indices and stronger dangling-OH infrared signatures, consistent with the formation of more porous ASW. We also show that pressure-based density estimates are unreliable in the background geometry because cryopumping decouple chamber pressure from the local substrate flux. For doser-grown films, optical interference imaging allows reconstruction of dome-shaped thickness profiles and estimation of effective densities. Finally, sudden restructuring events are observed in thick (above 2 micrometers), low-temperature porous ASW films, evidenced by abrupt changes in interferometric contrast, optical scattering, and dangling-OH features. These results establish COSPINU2 as a versatile platform for quantitative water-ice characterization and provide the physical baseline required for future studies of infrared irradiation and optical constants of interstellar ice analogues.

Abstract: The Radio Neutrino Observatory in Greenland (RNO-G) is designed to detect ultra-high-energy neutrinos by capturing the radio signals generated when these rare particles interact with glacial ice. Located near Summit Station on the Greenland ice sheet, RNO-G will ultimately consist of 35 autonomous stations, each spaced 1.25 km apart and equipped with arrays of in-ice antennas. A key component of each station is a set of three subsurface strings of antennas, which require boreholes that are approximately 100 meters deep and more than 285 mm wide in diameter. Construction of the observatory began in 2021, and eight stations have been deployed to date. To meet the drilling requirements, the project utilizes the BigRAID system. Developed through a collaboration between the University of Wisconsin-Madison, the British Antarctic Survey (BAS), and the RNO-G Collaboration, BigRAID is being continually refined to improve its reliability, enhance drilling speed, and incorporate advanced automation and control software. This paper will review recent upgrades to the drill and assess its performance in the field through the four seasons of drilling.

Abstract: We describe a framework for building low-rank separable expansions to model spectroscopic data from systems with time-varying spectra, in settings where the spectral dimension is finely sampled (in wavelength), but the temporal dimension is sparsely and possibly irregulary sampled. The motivating application is detection and characterization of small exoplanets, e.g., Earth-like planets around Sun-like stars. Such planets cannot be seen directly, but can be detected via the minute Doppler shifts of the host star spectrum due to the star's motion as the planet and star revolve around their mutual center of mass. Current instruments are reaching the precision necessary to detect the ~10 cm/s motions corresponding to an exo-Earth. But the small Doppler shifts from such low-amplitude orbital motion can be masked or mimicked by the effects of stellar activity on the surface of the host star. e.g., starspots (dark regions), faculae (bright filaments), etc. One technique astronomers have used to try to model the stellar activity component of the signal is PCA, applied to an ensemble of spectra collected over time. Abstract: We present pneo, a Bayesian classifier that computes the probability that a single-night tracklet belongs to a near-Earth object (NEO), using only its sky position, angular motion, and apparent magnitude. The result is the exact posterior probability under that model, with no tuned scores or separately chosen priors: its classifications are optimal for the adopted model, and its probabilities are calibrated.

The advantage grows at Rubin's depth, where the faint background of main-belt and more distant objects is still largely undiscovered. On simulated tracklets to that depth, its selection is 2 times purer than that of digest2 (96% against 48%). On the Rubin alert stream and Rubin Data Preview 2 its selection is 1.4-1.7 times purer than that of digest2, and its predicted NEO counts are close to those observed. By raising the fraction of follow-up targets that are NEOs, pneo could potentially double the effective capacity of NEO follow-up in the Rubin era.

Abstract: Spatially resolved galaxy properties are routinely derived from integral-field spectroscopy and increasingly from multi-band photometry as a wealth of photometric data become available in large surveys like LSST/Rubin. Spatially resolved star formation histories (SFHs) offer a detailed view of galaxy growth and quenching by tracing the timing and location of star formation within a galaxy up to one billion years in the past. However, stellar migration between spaxels may strongly bias these reconstructions. Quantifying this bias remains challenging as few Milky-Way mass simulations fully resolve initial stellar dispersions or migration rates. We use the well-validated h277 Milky-Way-mass cosmological zoom simulation, which resolves both via explicitly tying star formation to the non-equilibrium abundance of H2, to assess the quality of specific star formation rates (sSFR) radial profiles inferred from resolved SFHs against actual values from stellar particle tracking. Across several evolutionary windows spanning a range of stellar activity stages from quiescent to star-bursting, we find that the adaptive radial binning imposed by S/N constraints in photometric SED fitting actually mitigates most of the blurring/mixing effects of stellar migration on inferred radial profiles up to lookback times of 1.5 Gyr. This allows for proper recovery of not only the average sSFR but also its radial slope, its relative changes in scatter across radial annuli and even approximate localization of low-z star-forming clumps. Only galaxies having undergone a major merger across the considered lookback time display discrepant, unreliable sSFR profiles, hence should not be considered for such reconstructions.

Abstract: Variability in active galactic nuclei (AGNs) is remarkably diverse, spanning multiple timescales, wavelengths, and physical processes. Variability probes accretion physics and the interplay between supermassive black holes and their surroundings. Building on early case studies, the field has shifted toward population-level analyses of increasingly large datasets, making scalable techniques crucial for upcoming time-domain surveys. We developed a Python-based pipeline to analyze AGN light curves using variability metrics and applied it to 14,061 nearby AGNs ($z\lesssim 0.09$) observed with ZTF. We examined metric distributions and correlations. Amplitude-based metrics (e.g., $F_{\rm var}$) help identify major flares but are sensitive to single deviant epochs. Temporal metrics such as $η$ provide a complementary check. Comparison with WISE infrared photometry suggests that $η$ traces the strength of AGN activity. Most Type~I and Type~II AGNs in our sample exhibit similar variability patterns based on these metrics. This overlap may partly reflect misclassifications that targeted spectroscopic follow-up could help resolve. To identify subsets of AGNs with distinctive variability, we used UMAP to group sources with similar metric values. The resulting embedding shows a region enriched in high-amplitude variables, including a subset with long-term flux trends. We find evidence that extremely variable quasars and changing-look AGNs may populate this region, which can help prioritize sources for follow-up. Our pipeline offers a scalable, survey-agnostic framework suitable for application to Vera C. Rubin Observatory's LSST and other ongoing and upcoming photometric surveys.

We investigate the environments of two galaxies from the Atacama Large Millimeter/submillimeter Array (ALMA) CRISTAL survey: CRISTAL-19 (C19; $z=5.235$) and CRISTAL-21 (C21; $z=5.256$), using F444W slitless spectroscopy from the Near-Infrared Camera (NIRCam) on the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) in COSMOS-3D. A friends-of-friends analysis of H$α$ emitters identifies two distinct overdense nodes separated by approximately 25 comoving Mpc. Although C21 is the more massive galaxy, C19 has the more massive, actively star-forming companions. Abstract: We investigate a method for inferring the local dark matter (DM) density in the solar neighbourhood using a deep learning framework that learns the mapping between stellar kinematics and the underlying DM density field from cosmological simulations. Abstract: Through a retrospective search for tidal disruption events (TDEs) in the ZTF archives agnostic to their location within their host galaxies, we identify AT2024dky/ZTF24aagksvd as a promising off-nuclear TDE candidate. At $δt=858$ d, Keck reveals $M_{u'}=-13.07\pm0.09$ mag and $M_R > -14.15$ mag. At $δt=866$ d, VLA detects the transient at $νL_{ν,10\,\rm GHz}=(1.26\pm0.17)\times10^{38}\,\mathrm{erg\,s^{-1}}$. Across the sample, the trunks are extended, massive, and strongly bound reservoirs, while the leaves are significantly more massive, denser, and more tightly bound than isolated structures of similar spatial scale. To model the infrared emission, many modern SED-fitting codes rely on dust energy balance, which assumes that all energy absorbed by dust in the UV-optical is equal to the energy emitted by dust in the infrared regime. Simulations are performed in a 3D periodic box using the Phantom code. The gas probability distributions are consistent with a lognormal distribution for both methods and grain sizes. The dust distributions are different in the two methods, showing discrepancies particularly in regions of low density. The most significant difference between the two methods is in the dust-to-gas ratio distributions. Both methods peak at the mean dust-to-gas ratio of 0.01, but the two-fluid method has wider distributions than the one-fluid method suggesting excess dust concentration in dense filaments. The narrowing of the one-fluid distribution is also caused by the stopping time limiter used to maintain the terminal velocity approximation. This artificially makes the mixture more coupled in low-density regions. Abstract: We construct the black hole infrared triangle of scalar QED on a stretched Schwarzschild horizon at tree level. In the covariant phase space of the near-horizon Maxwell field, the soft charge is a corner term. The memory is the zero mode of the gauge-invariant radiative field along the generators. It is conjugate to the boundary gauge mode that labels the degenerate horizon vacua, and Gauss' law fixes it by the flux of charge through the horizon. The Noether flux of the residual gauge transformation through the stretched horizon is not conserved by the scattering, because charged matter crosses the stretched horizon in both directions. The flux with its matter part removed is the soft charge, which generates the large gauge transformation. Its Ward identity is equivalent to a soft photon theorem in every radiative partial wave, which we derive from the near-horizon Feynman rules and, where these do not control the emission, from the field equations at leading order. The main difference from null infinity is that on a stretched horizon both radial branches of the field are independent boundary data. The hard charge is a radial flux. With both branches present it weights each leg by the direction of its radial motion, and the soft factor is a sign per leg. The endpoint data on the two branches give two boundary observables, the time-integrated transverse magnetic and electric fields. The field radiated by a scattering process contributes only to the first, where it cancels the change in the Coulomb memory of the charges, so that the soft charge is conserved. A charge that crosses the horizon enters both. Abstract: The solutions of $D=4$ $\mathcal N=8$ SO(8)-gauged supergravity uplift consistently to eleven dimensions. All known supersymmetric AdS$_4$ vacua of this theory lie in an $\mathcal N=1$ truncation containing seven chiral multiplets. We then specialise to the $\mathcal N=1$ vacua with U(1)$^2$ and SO(3) symmetry, obtain their new $D=11$ uplifts and bring out their internal geometries. Both of them admit a Kaluza--Klein-type description. For the U(1)$^2$ solution, this uses the global Hopf fibration of $S^7$ over $\mathbb{CP}^3$. One U(1) acts on the fibre, while the other generates the only continuous isometry of the deformed $\mathbb{CP}^3$ base. First, we compute the holographic static quark-antiquark potential following the standard prescription that relates this boundary observable to the on shell-action of a string configuration whose endpoints are anchored at the boundary. We find that the screening length decreases both when the angular momentum is increased at fixed temperature and when the temperature is increased at fixed angular momentum. The former reflects centrifugal effects on the $q\bar{q}$ pair, whereas the latter stems from enhanced thermal fluctuations, which weaken correlations at a given separation. Second, we compute the growth rate of the Krylov complexity, using the holographic prescription that relates this observable to the momentum of a massive particle probe falling into the IR. Abstract: In this pedagogical article we discuss the Dirac equation in arbitrary coordinate systems. We explain the need for connections when dealing with spinor fields and present the practical steps needed to compute the Dirac operator in a general setting, allowing for arbitrary coordinate systems and position-dependent orthonormal gamma matrices, both of which can simplify computations in certain scenarios. The extension to include minimally coupled gravity is straightforward. Examples are provided, including constructing the Dirac equation in spherical and cylindrical symmetry (with or without gravity) and the Kerr spacetime. We also briefly comment on the advantages and disadvantages of various tetrads and orthonormal gamma matrices. This manuscript should be accessible to readers who have studied the Dirac equation at an introductory level (e.g., in Cartesian coordinates in flat spacetime with constant gamma matrices). The emphasis is on the use of orthonormal frames, since these correspond to the coordinate systems most familiar to readers when dealing with most field theories, and represent the physical frames where measurements are performed. The aim is to get the reader quickly to the point of being able to do calculations, so the presentation favors a concise, pragmatic approach over extended derivations.

Abstract: We study late-time out-of-time-order correlators (OTOCs) of semi-degenerate primary fields in two-dimensional $A_{N-1}$ Toda conformal field theory. For $N=3$, a transverse contour pinch reduces the spectral integral to one dimension. Assuming that the reduced expansion is complete, in the continuous phase the regulated edge behaves as a discrete state, and the OTOC approaches a pure braiding phase. As we check numerically, the edge block is a free-field correlator in a charge-neutral Weyl frame; this explains the pure phase and is consistent with crossing symmetry. For both $N=2$ and $N=3$, a dominant discrete residue gives a constant late-time limit. For $N\geq4$, the same one-dimensional reduction violates a crossing bound on the lowest intermediate weight of a positive diagonal expansion. Hence the $N=2,3$ phase diagram does not extend to higher rank without additional contributions below the reduced continuum edge or outside a positive diagonal expansion.

We demonstrate how these two mechanisms resolve the puzzle in two concrete examples in 4d $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM, where we consider two-point correlators in the presence of a $\frac{1}{2}$-BPS Wilson line and on $\mathbb{RP}^4$.

Abstract: We outline the history of the research of dynamic systems including higher derivatives in the Lagrangian, The story starts in 1744 when Euler published a book where the Euler--Lagrange differential equations for a HD system were first written. The next important landmarks were the papers of Ostrogradsky (1850) and of Pais and Uhlenbeck (1950). Many articles written in the second half of the last century were influenced by a misconception that was widespread at the time --- it was believed that higher derivatives necessarily bring about a violation of unitarity.
  We understand today that this is not the case and there are many systems with benign ghosts where HD are present but unitarity is not violated. In this review, we present and discuss several examples of such (quantum) mechanical systems and 2D field theories. Then we will briefly discuss higher-derivative gravity theories in 4 dimensions. In that case, the question whether the ghosts there are benign or not has a special status due to the absence of a nonperturbative Hamiltonian and does not have a definite answer yet. However, if one restricts oneself to classical theory, one can argue that the ghosts {\it are} benign (benign classical ghostly system is a system that needs an infinite time to reach a singularity, and it seems to the case both in ordinary and in HD gravity: the cosmic censorship conjecture implies that the singularities are always surrounded by horizons, and it takes an infinite time on the clocks of a distant observer to reach a horizon).
  Finally, I discuss supersymmetric HD theories: conformal supergravity, where the ghosts are also probably benign, and then an interesting supersymmetric HD gauge theory in 6 dimensions. It involves a dimensionless coupling constant, is renormalizable and rather beautiful. Unfortunately, it is not unitary --- ghosts are not benign there.

</details>

Abstract: We show that in any $d$-dimensional CFT, the vacuum sandwiched Rényi mutual information (SRMI) of integer order $n$ between two disjoint balls maps to a CFT partition function on a manifold of topology $\#^{n-1}(S^1 \times S^{d-1})$, with moduli fixed by the ball configuration and single-ball modular flow. At $n=2$, it is given by the thermal partition function on $S^{d-1}$, upper-bounding the von Neumann mutual information. Abstract: Parallel Tempering on Boundary Conditions (PTBC) mitigates topological freezing by exchanging configurations between replicas that interpolate between periodic and open boundaries on a small defect. We generalize PTBC by replacing the instantaneous swap with a short out-of-equilibrium protocol, in which the defect couplings of two replicas are exchanged gradually while the fields around the defect are updated. We show that the swap rate depends only on the average work spent along the out-of-equilibrium trajectory by virtue of Crooks' and Jarzynski's fluctuation relations. From the theoretical derivation of the scaling of the work as a function of the number of replicas, protocol length, and defect size, we predict the swap rate and the optimal protocol length. These predictions are tested in the $2d$ $\mathrm{CP}^{N-1}$ model with $N=21$. The optimal setup agrees with the measured one, the total computational cost at fixed statistical precision decreases by $\gtrsim40\%$ with respect to standard PTBC for all explored lattice spacings, and the required number of replicas at fixed acceptance is halved.

The field strength insertions in generalized Wilson loops are renormalized using gradient flow, which also leads to small statistical uncertainties. All potentials are extrapolated to zero flow time and, in contrast to previous lattice studies, to the continuum, using three lattice spacings and four flow times. The spin dependent potentials are matched to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme at NLO and provided at $μ= 3 \, \text{GeV}$. Abstract: We investigate how physics between the grand-unification and Planck scales affects neutrino mixing in the standard three-flavor framework, distinguishing genuine non unitarity from unitary shifts of the mixing parameters. First, we show that heavy gauge singlet states at or above the grand-unification scale induce deviations from unitarity of the effective (3\times3) Pontecorvo Maki Nakagawa Sakata (PMNS) matrix, suppressed by the ratio of the light-neutrino mass to the heavy mass. The resulting effects are estimated to be of order (10^{-27}), far below current oscillation constraints. Second, we examine the impact of a flavor-blind dimension-five Weinberg operator suppressed by a high-energy scale (Λ). This operator modifies the neutrino mass matrix and shifts the mixing angles while preserving the exact unitarity of the PMNS matrix. Using the NuFIT 6.1 best-fit parameters for normal mass ordering, including the Dirac CP-violating phase, and numerically diagonalizing the corrected complex-symmetric mass matrix, we find that the shift in (θ_{12}) can reach approximately (0.12^\circ) at the Planck scale for a quasi-degenerate neutrino spectrum, compared with (0.011^\circ) for normal ordering. The corrections increase as (1/Λ), reaching approximately (6.5^\circ) for normal ordering near the grand-unification scale, where perturbation theory becomes unreliable for the quasi-degenerate spectrum. Since the Weinberg operator acts entirely within the active three-flavor sector, it introduces no additional non-unitarity parameters; instead, the correlated shifts of the three mixing angles are governed by a single parameter. Our results demonstrate that tests of active-flavor probability conservation cannot directly constrain this high-scale contribution. Its phenomenological signatures must instead be sought in the correlated modifications of the neutrino mixing parameters.

</details>

Abstract: We present the first direct tensor network calculation of flavor-resolved parton distribution functions (PDFs) for a pion-like isospin triplet in the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model in (1+1) dimensions. Four-fermion interactions bind the mesons without confinement. Using staggered fermions and a charge-symmetrized Hamiltonian, we prepare the bound states and compute light-cone correlators by real-time evolution. The resulting quark and antiquark PDFs obey the expected isospin relations under $u \leftrightarrow d$ exchange between charged pions and exhibit equal u and d distributions in the neutral pion. A flavor-independent antisymmetrization of the field ordering removes the vacuum-subtracted anticommutator contribution and restores charge-conjugation relations in both the correlators and PDFs across the triplet within numerical accuracy. These results provide benchmarks for quantum simulations of the partonic structure of the meson.

The same hard component can mimic a higher core temperature or thermal-pion emission in $γ$-ray spectral fits. The $Δ(1232)$ resonance is therefore crucial for predicting supernova axion signals and interpreting their spectra.

Abstract: We develop a physics-informed neural-network (PINN) framework for solving the rainbow-truncated quark Dyson--Schwinger equation at real and complex momenta.
